# The Book of Giants — Critical Edition

> **Source**: Database of Manichaean Texts (DbMT 2025) — manichaeism.de
> **Editor**: Samuel N.C. Lieu FBA
> **Pages**: 122
>
> Mani's Book of Giants: fragments in Middle Persian, Sogdian, Uyghur, Coptic, and Parthian. Critical edition with original scripts and translations.

---

UNION ACADÉMIQUE INTERNATIONALE (UAI) INTERNATIONAL UNION OF ACADEMIES (IUA)
Project 67: Corpus Fontium Manichaeorum DATABASE OF MANICHAEAN TEXTS (eDbMT)
THE ART OF THE MANICHAEAN SCRIBE
II
BOOK OF THE GIANTS
Text fragments published by W.B. Henning FBA Werner Sundermann FBA
Supplemented by those published by Enrico Morano Jens Wilkens
Compiled by
Samuel N.C. Lieu FBA
ofiar infrared

---

THE BOOK OF THE GIANTS
Text and translation based mainly on
W. B. Henning, 'The Book of the Giants' BSOAS 11/1 (1943) 52-74.
With additional textual and photographic material from:
W. B. Henning, 'Ein Manichäisches Henochbuch', SPAW, 1934, 27 -.
E. Morano, 'Some new Sogdian fragments related to Mani's Book of Giants and the Problem of the influence of Jewish Enochich literature', in M. Goff et al. (eds.) Ancient Tales of Giants from Qumran and Turfan WUNT 360 (Tübingen, 2015), 187-198.
P.O. Skjærvø, 'Iranian Epic and the Manichean Book of Giants. Irano-Manichaica III', Acta Oreintalia ... Hungaricae, 48 (1995), 187-223.
W. Sundermann, 'Ein weiteres Fragment aus Manis Gigantenbuch', Orientalia J. Duchesne- Guillemin emerito oblata, Acta Iranica 23, 2nd Ser. 9 (1984) 491-505.
W. Sundermann, 'Mani's "Book of the Giants" and the Jewish Books of Enoch. A case of terminological difference and what it implies', in S. Shaked and A. Netzer, Irano-Judaica III (Jerusalem, 1994) 39-49.
J. Wilkens, 'Neue Fragmente aus Manis Gigantenbuch', Zeitschrift der Deutschen Morgenländischen Gesellschaft, 150 (2000) 133-76.
ABBREVIATIONS
BSOAS = Bulletin of the School of Oriental and African Studies London)
ED = G. Clauson, An Etymological Dictionary of Pre-Thirteenth Century Turkish, Oxford, 1972.
HdA = J. Wilkens, Handwörterbuch des Altuigurischen (Göttingen, 2021)
IFOC = K. Kudara, S. Sundermann and Y. Yoshida (eds.), Iranian Fragments from the Ôtani Collection, 2 vols. (Kyoto, 1997).
IMTT i = W. Sundermann (ed.), Iranian Manichaean Turfan Texts in early publications (1904-1934), Photo Edition, Corpus Inscriptionum Iranicarum, Suppl. Ser. III (London, 1996).
KPT = W. Sundermann, Mittelpersische und parthische kosmogonische und Parabletexte der Manichäer, BT 8 (Berlin, 1973).
SPAW = Sitzungsberichte der preussischen Akademie der Wissenschaften zu Berlin (Berlin, 1882- 1921; philos .- hist. Kl., 1922-49).
WBH = W. B. Henning, 'The Book of the Giants', BSOAS 11/1 (1943) 52-74.

And the story about the Great Fire: like unto (the way in which) the Fire, with powerful wrath, swallows the world and enjoys it; like unto (the way in which) this fire that is in (25) the body, swallows the exterior fire that is (lit. comes) in fruit and food, and enjoys it. Again, like unto (the story in which) two brothers who found a treasure, and a pursuer lacerated each other, and they died; like unto (the fight in which) Ohya, Lewyātīn (= Leviathan), and Raphael lacerated each other, and they vanished; like unto (the story in which) a lion cub, a calf in a wood (or: on a meadow), and a fox lacerated each other, [and they vanished, or: died]. Now attend and behold how the Great King of Honour who is Evvota, is in the third heavan. He is ... with the wrath ... and a rebellion ... , when malice and wrath arose in his camp, namely the Egreegoroi of Heaven who in his watch-district (rebelled and) descended to the earth. They did all deeds of malice. They revealed the arts in the world, and the mysteries of heaven to the men. Rebellion and ruin came about on earth ...

81

---

THE BOOK OF THE GIANTS
وكتاب سفر الجبابرة لماني البابلىّ مُلوء من قصص هؤلاء الجبابرة الذين منهم سام ونريمان وكأنّة قد أخذ هذين الاسمين من كتاب افدستاك لزرداشت الآذربيجانىّ وكذا قول الهند في مجئ باسديو المرسّل لاصالح العالم وأفناء الجبابرة في وقت بهارث وقت عمل بياس بن يرابش كتابا مشتمال على مائة وعشرين الفأ من الابيات بأوزانهم كلّها في اقاصيص تلك الجبابرة من حروبهم واحوالهم
The Book of the Giants by Mani the Babylonian is full of the stories of these giants, which included Sam and Narīman, two names that he may have borrowed from the book Avesta by Zoroaster in Azerbaijan.
Likewise, the Indians have a tradition of the coming of Vasudeva, who at the time of the Bharata was sent to put the world in order and destroy the giants.
Vyasa the son of بيرابش (Parashara) wrote a book containing 120,000 verses in their meters, all dealing with the stories of these giants, their wars, and other situations.

(WBH, p. 72)

THE BOOK OF THE GIANTS
Parallels from the Ethiopic Book of Enoch: Trans. M. A. Knibb, The Ethiopic Book of Enoch (Oxford, 1978), 105-108:
1Enoch, 18: 12-16: And beyond this chasm I saw a place and (it had) neither the firmament of heaven above it, nor the foundation of earth below it; there was no water on it, and no birds, but it was a desert place. And a terrible thing I saw there - seven stars like great burning mountains. And like a spirit questioning me the angel said: 'This is the place of the end of heaven and earth; this is the prison for the stars of heaven and the host of heaven. And the stars which ro, these are the ones which transgressed the command of the Lord from the beginning of their rising because they did not come out at their proper times. And he was angry with them and bound them until the time of the consummation of their sin in the year of mystery.
1Enoch, 21: 1-6 And I went around to a place where there was nothing made. And I saw there a terrible thing - neither the high heaven, nor the (firmly) founded earth, but a desert place, prepared and terrible. And there I saw seven stars of heaven bound on it together, like great mountains, and burning like fire. Then I said: 'For what sin have they been bound, and why have they been thrown here?' and Uriel, one of the holy angels who was with me and led me, spoke to me and said: 'Enoch, about whom do you ask? About whom do you enquire and ask and care? These are (some) of the stars which transgressed the command of the Lord Most High, and they have been bound here until ten thousand ages are completed, the number of the days of their sins".

wyn, ZKn article 'the', primarily gen.sg.m., but also used for gen. without regard to number or gender and occasionally as a general obl. form (e.g. KB9(1), KB11(1), KG522-4,TaleAS15 as acc .; HC36.6V8 as loc.). 'xš ywny, 'xšyw'ny n.m. 'king, ruler'. The Father of Greatness is referred to by titles such as 'xšywny Byy 'king god' (Sundermann 2001, 128 with n. 208 on p. 155) or mzyx 'xšywny 'great king'. In TIIB 8 he is the 'yqwncyq xšywny 'everlasting king' or nwšy xšywny 'eternal king'.

ptycy, S pt'yc, ptyc, pt'ycy postp., rarely prep. 'before, rhnd, S rx'nt n. 'arhant'. Indian loanword. in the presence of, to meet'. Also used as the rhnd KawIV6* designation of divine beings who come to meet the righteous soul after death, see Sundermann 2001, 835-6; Reck 2003, 337 n. 89; Yoshida 2010a, 701. The '3 ptycyh', who are mentioned in M583iiV16, probably also in M583iiR13 and M6100.2, may thus be identified with the 'three Amesha Spentas' ryj KawZ18 = M7800iiR18 of M6132B2, the 'three gods' of the parallel Arabic and Turkish accounts (see s.v. mro'spnd and Reck 2003, 330-31).

-š pers.pron.3.sg.encl. 'him, his, her, it, its'. Rarely found in non-encl. usage as the first word of a clause. The principal forms attested are -šw (chiefly šw-v.itr. 'to go; to act, behave', past stem S yt -. On yt- acc., but occasionally used for gen.) and -šy (obl., as past stem of šw- see BT XII, 164, 226-7; XIX, 135. According to GMS §603 (following Tedesco 1923, 35) the past stem is xrt, but see s.v. xyr. šwo' 2.pl.impv. KawZ6 = M7800iiR16 šx- a. 'hard, solid, firm' used for acc. and gen.). In addition, the forms -š- (abl., with postp. - c or -8) and -'š (used for gen.) are occasionally attested. GMS §1365-74. S -cy in 20230V1 (rty-cy-By) is presumably a variant of -šy with c representing the combination of š with the šxyy nom.sg.m. KawG36 (as acc.) preceding t. šxmyz't 'Šahmīzād', name of a giant.
